People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I guess I’m here because work has been feeling… harder than it should, maybe. The district added this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout to my plate, on top of my regular teaching, and I’m still managing to get through the school day, so it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything.

But I do feel more wound up and discouraged than I’m used to, and I’ve noticed I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would’ve just handled. It’s just not really like me, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.

It’s just not really like me, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are you still enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I think… mostly, yes. I mean, I haven’t really taken stock of it, honestly. I still like the things I like, as far as I can tell. Maybe I’m just a little less quick to get to them lately, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying them. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think “on edge” is a fair way to put it. I wouldn’t say I’m panicking or anything like that, and I’m still getting through my school day, but since the district added this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on top of my regular teaching, I do feel more wound up than usual.\n\nAnd discouraged, too, honestly. I’ve noticed I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would’ve just handled without thinking much about it. It’s just not very like me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad lately?\n\nPatient: Sad… maybe a little, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still getting through my teaching day and doing what I need to do. But since this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout got added on top of my regular work, I’ve felt more discouraged than I’m used to.\n\nIt’s not like me exactly—I’ve been putting off lesson plans and emails I normally would’ve just handled. So I guess I’d say discouraged more than deeply sad, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t think there’s been any noticeable change. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any tasks or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little. I mean, I’m not just ignoring everything, I am still getting through my school day. But I’ve been putting off lesson plans and emails lately, which is unusual for me.\n\nIt really seemed to start after the district added that bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on top of my regular teaching. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I feel kind of wound up and discouraged, and those tasks that I normally would’ve handled without a second thought just feel harder to start. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or certain people because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I might be a little quieter than usual sometimes, but I’m not avoiding certain people or canceling things because of it. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Does that seem like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually do them. Maybe I’m a little less eager to start sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped finding pleasure in them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling wound up and discouraged?\n\nPatient: Probably about five months now… not forever or anything, but since the district assigned me that rollout role midyear. That’s when I started feeling more wound up and discouraged. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess I’m here because work has been feeling… harder than it should, maybe. The district added this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout to my plate, on top of my regular teaching, and I’m still managing to get through the school day, so it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything.\n\nBut I do feel more wound up and discouraged than I’m used to, and I’ve noticed I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would’ve just handled. It’s just not really like me, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are you still enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I think… mostly, yes. I mean, I haven’t really taken stock of it, honestly. I still like the things I like, as far as I can tell. Maybe I’m just a little less quick to get to them lately, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying them. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think “on edge” is a fair way to put it. I wouldn’t say I’m panicking or anything like that, and I’m still getting through my school day, but since the district added this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on top of my regular teaching, I do feel more wound up than usual.\n\nAnd discouraged, too, honestly. I’ve noticed I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would’ve just handled without thinking much about it. It’s just not very like me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad lately?\n\nPatient: Sad… maybe a little, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still getting through my teaching day and doing what I need to do. But since this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout got added on top of my regular work, I’ve felt more discouraged than I’m used to.\n\nIt’s not like me exactly—I’ve been putting off lesson plans and emails I normally would’ve just handled. So I guess I’d say discouraged more than deeply sad, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t think there’s been any noticeable change. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any tasks or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little. I mean, I’m not just ignoring everything, I am still getting through my school day. But I’ve been putting off lesson plans and emails lately, which is unusual for me.\n\nIt really seemed to start after the district added that bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on top of my regular teaching. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I feel kind of wound up and discouraged, and those tasks that I normally would’ve handled without a second thought just feel harder to start. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or certain people because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I might be a little quieter than usual sometimes, but I’m not avoiding certain people or canceling things because of it. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Does that seem like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually do them. Maybe I’m a little less eager to start sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped finding pleasure in them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling wound up and discouraged?\n\nPatient: Probably about five months now… not forever or anything, but since the district assigned me that rollout role midyear. That’s when I started feeling more wound up and discouraged. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're just going through the motions of daily life without much emotional response?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can have days where I’m just doing what needs to get done, but I still feel like myself, and I still react to things. I wouldn’t say I’m numb or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um… not especially, I don’t think. Is that okay—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you usually find interesting or enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a major way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not dramatically. I’m 56, so sometimes I figure being tired is just part of a busy week, especially with teaching. I wouldn’t say it’s been a big change or anything concerning. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling less emotionally responsive to things that would normally affect you, like good or bad news?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I still react to things—if something good happens I’m happy, and if something upsetting happens, it does get to me. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess I’m here because work has been feeling… harder than it should, maybe. The district added this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout to my plate, on top of my regular teaching, and I’m still managing to get through the school day, so it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything.\n\nBut I do feel more wound up and discouraged than I’m used to, and I’ve noticed I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would’ve just handled. It’s just not really like me, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are you still enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I think… mostly, yes. I wouldn’t say I’m panicking or anything like that, and I’m still getting through my school day, but since the district added this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on top of my regular teaching, I do feel more wound up than usual.\n\nAnd discouraged, too, honestly. I’ve noticed I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would’ve just handled without thinking much about it. It’s just not very like me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad lately?\n\nPatient: Sad… maybe a little, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still getting through my teaching day and doing what I need to do. But since this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout got added on top of my regular work, I’ve felt more discouraged than I’m used to.\n\nIt’s not like me exactly—I’ve been putting off lesson plans and emails I normally would’ve just handled. So I guess I’d say discouraged more than deeply sad, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t think there’s been any noticeable change. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any tasks or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little. I mean, I’m not just ignoring everything, I am still getting through my school day. But I’ve been putting off lesson plans and emails lately, which is unusual for me.\n\nIt really seemed to start after the district added that bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on top of my regular teaching. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I feel kind of wound up and discouraged, and those tasks that I normally would’ve handled without a second thought just feel harder to start. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or certain people because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I might be a little quieter than usual sometimes, but I’m not avoiding certain people or canceling things because of it. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Does that seem like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling wound up and discouraged?\n\nPatient: Probably about five months now… not forever or anything, but since the district assigned me that rollout role midyear. That’s when I started feeling more wound up and discouraged. I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually make time for them. Maybe I’m a little less eager some days, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not dramatically. I’m 56, so sometimes I figure being tired is just part of a busy week, especially with teaching. I wouldn’t say it’s been a big change or anything concerning. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling less emotionally responsive to things that would normally affect you, like good or bad news?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I still react to things—if something good happens I’m happy, and if something upsetting happens, it does get to me. Maybe I try to keep things steady, especially at work, but I wouldn’t say I feel numb or anything like that. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling disconnected from your emotions or the people around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I feel kind of wound up and discouraged, and those tasks that I normally would’ve handled without a second thought just feel harder to start. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or certain people because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I might be a little quieter than usual sometimes, but I’m not avoiding certain people or canceling things because of it. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Does that seem like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually do them. Maybe I’m a little less eager to start sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped finding pleasure in them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling wound up and discouraged?\n\nPatient: Probably about five months now… not forever or anything, but since the district assigned me that rollout role midyear. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um… not especially, I don’t think. I mean, I can second-guess myself sometimes, like whether I handled something the best way, but I wouldn’t say I’m carrying around a lot of guilt. Is that okay—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you usually find interesting or enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a major way. I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually make time for them. Maybe I’m a little less eager some days, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not dramatically. I’m 56, so sometimes I figure being tired is just part of a busy week, especially with teaching. I wouldn’t say it’s been a big change or anything concerning. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling less emotionally responsive to things that would normally affect you, like good or bad news?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I still react to things—if something good happens I’m happy, and if something upsetting happens, it does get to me. Maybe I try to keep things steady, especially at work, but I wouldn’t say I feel numb or anything like that. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling disconnected from your emotions or the people around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I still feel connected to people around me, and I can tell what I’m feeling most of the time. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling wound up and discouraged?\n\nPatient: Probably about five months now… not forever or anything, but since the district assigned me that rollout role midyear. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um… not especially, I don’t think. I mean, I can second-guess myself sometimes, like whether I handled something the best way, but I wouldn’t say I’m carrying around a lot of guilt. Is that okay—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you usually find interesting or enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a major way. I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually make time for them. Maybe I’m a little less eager some days, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not dramatically. I’m 56, so sometimes I figure being tired is just part of a busy week, especially with teaching. I wouldn’t say it’s been a big change or anything concerning. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling less emotionally responsive to things that would normally affect you, like good or bad news?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I still react to things—if something good happens I’m happy, and if something upsetting happens, it does get to me. Maybe I try to keep things steady, especially at work, but I wouldn’t say I feel numb or anything like that. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling disconnected from your emotions or the people around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I still feel connected to people around me, and I can tell what I’m feeling most of the time. Maybe I keep things to myself a bit, but I wouldn’t say I feel disconnected, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy doing most of the time over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Not really, I don’t think so. I still enjoy the things I usually like when I get the chance. Maybe some days I don’t get around to them as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest most of the time. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling wound up and discouraged?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. That’s when I started feeling more wound up and discouraged. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're just going through the motions of daily life without much emotional response?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can have days where I’m just doing what needs to get done, but I still feel like myself, and I still react to things. I wouldn’t say I’m numb or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um… not especially, I don’t think. I mean, I can second-guess myself sometimes, like whether I handled something the best way, but I wouldn’t say I’m carrying around a lot of guilt. Is that okay—does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you usually find interesting or enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a major way. I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually make time for them. Maybe I’m a little less eager some days, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not dramatically. I’m 56, so sometimes I figure being tired is just part of a busy week, especially with teaching. I wouldn’t say it’s been a big change or anything concerning. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling less emotionally responsive to things that would normally affect you, like good or bad news?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I still react to things—if something good happens I’m happy, and if something upsetting happens, it does get to me. Maybe I try to keep things steady, especially at work, but I wouldn’t say I feel numb or anything like that. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling disconnected from your emotions or the people around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I still feel connected to people around me, and I can tell what I’m feeling most of the time. Maybe I keep things to myself a bit, but I wouldn’t say I feel disconnected, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy doing most of the time over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Not really, I don’t think so. I still enjoy the things I usually like when I get the chance. Maybe some days I don’t get around to them as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest most of the time. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling wound up and discouraged?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. My weight’s been about the same as far as I can tell. Maybe it goes up or down a little like normal, but nothing significant that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your wound-up and discouraged feelings have been interfering with your relationships or daily activities in a significant way?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still teaching and I am getting the basic things done. But I’ve noticed I’m not as on top of planning as I usually am. I’ve missed or pushed back a few planning deadlines, and a couple of colleagues have had to remind me about things I used to just handle on my own. So… it is affecting my day-to-day work some, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m still getting through my teaching day and doing what I need to do. I mean, I’m not just ignoring everything, I am still getting through my school day. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I feel kind of wound up and discouraged, and those tasks that I normally would’ve handled without a second thought just feel harder to start. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or certain people because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I might be a little quieter than usual sometimes, but I’m not avoiding certain people or canceling things because of it. At least, I haven’t really noticed that. Does that seem like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling wound up and discouraged?\n\nPatient: Probably about five months now… not forever or anything, but since the district assigned me that rollout role midyear. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not dramatically. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling less emotionally responsive to things that would normally affect you, like good or bad news?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling disconnected from your emotions or the people around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so.
